- **orienting**: Get up to speed in an unfamiliar codebase before any change is chosen — prove the build, test and lint commands by running them, find the conventions actually in force, the hot paths, and what nobody tests. Use when arriving in a client repo or a project you have not worked in, or when asked what this codebase does, how it is built, or where to start. Produces understanding, and stops before planning a change. Triggers on orient, get up to speed, unfamiliar repo, what does this codebase do, or where do I start. - **orienting-codebases**: Interactive codebase orientation for a HUMAN who wants to learn the code. Runs the same tree-sitting + featuring pipeline as exploring-codebases but synthesizes it into guided exercises and an HTML teaching artifact rather than an analysis document. Use for "orient me to this repo", "teach me this codebase", "walk me through this code", "learning orientation", or when someone wants genuine comprehension rather than a task completed. The audience is the test: if nobody is being taught and the goal is to get work done, use exploring-codebases instead. - **orienting-for-fdm**: Chooses the print orientation of an FDM/FFF part: which way up to lay it on the bed to minimize overhangs and support, maximize bed adhesion, keep it short and stable, and, when a load direction is given, keep that load along the layers rather than across them (the weak direction). Use when deciding how to orient a part for printing, reducing supports, improving first-layer adhesion, or trading orientation against strength, and as the step between generating a part and verifying it. Rests the part on each convex-hull face and scores the candidates; composes only trimesh and numpy, with no ext… ([Eli-code1/printagent](https://github.com/Eli-code1/printagent/tree/HEAD/skills/orienting-for-fdm))

Use whenever a question touches past decisions, architecture choices, team practices, project status, or an entity key. - **ors-contribution-framing**: Use when articulating the contribution of an Operations Research (OR) manuscript — especially the mandatory cover-letter contribution statement (since 1 June 2023, fewer than 500 words) and the discussion's significance claims. Frames why the work matters to OR; it does not position against specific prior papers (ors-literature-positioning) or run experiments (ors-data-analysis). ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Operations-Research-Skills/skills/ors-contribution-framing) · ★1.4k · 3 copies)
- **ors-data-analysis**: Use when running and reporting the computational study for an Operations Research (OR) manuscript — benchmark instances, baselines, reproducible experiments, statistical care for stochastic output, and the ORJournal code-and-data reproducibility workflow. Executes and reports the numerical evidence; it does not prove the results (ors-methods) or lay out the exhibits (ors-tables-figures). ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Operations-Research-Skills/skills/ors-data-analysis) · ★1.4k · 3 copies)
- **ors-literature-positioning**: Use when placing an Operations Research (OR) manuscript against the OR/MS literature — separating your model, results, and algorithmic guarantees from the closest prior work so the novelty is unambiguous. Positions the contribution; it does not formulate the model (ors-theory-development) or write the contribution statement (ors-contribution-framing). ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Operations-Research-Skills/skills/ors-literature-positioning) · ★1.4k · 3 copies)
- **ors-methods**: Use when designing the proof technique, algorithm, or simulation protocol for an Operations Research (OR) manuscript — choosing the right machinery (duality, dynamic programming, probabilistic coupling, convergence analysis, simulation output analysis) to actually establish the claimed results. Establishes the results; it does not state the model (ors-theory-development) or run the experiments (ors-data-analysis). ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Operations-Research-Skills/skills/ors-methods) · ★1.4k · 3 copies)
- **ors-rebuttal**: Use when responding to an Operations Research (OR) decision letter — writing the point-by-point response, closing proof gaps, adding baselines/instances, and passing the ORJournal code/data reproducibility review across a revision cycle. Drafts the revision and response; it does not parse the decision in the first place (ors-review-process) or run the final preflight (ors-submission). ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Operations-Research-Skills/skills/ors-rebuttal) · ★1.4k · 3 copies)
- **ors-review-process**: Use when navigating the Operations Research (OR) editorial process — the departmental area-editor routing, the soft double-anonymous model with asymmetric transparency, decision types, and how to read a decision letter from the handling Area Editor. Explains how review works and how to read a decision; it does not draft the response (ors-rebuttal) or run the preflight (ors-submission). ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Operations-Research-Skills/skills/ors-review-process) · ★1.4k · 3 copies)
- **ors-submission**: Use when running the final pre-submission preflight for an Operations Research (OR) manuscript — INFORMS Author Portal/ScholarOne, area selection, recommending Associate Editors and reviewers, soft double-anonymous anonymization, the mandatory contribution statement, page-tier limits, and the code/data deposit plan. Checks readiness to submit; it does not handle the post-decision response (ors-rebuttal). ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Operations-Research-Skills/skills/ors-submission) · ★1.4k · 3 copies)
- **ors-tables-figures**: Use when building the exhibits for an Operations Research (OR) manuscript — theorem/assumption layout, comparison tables against prior work, computational-results tables, and convergence/scaling plots in INFORMS house style. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Operations-Research-Skills/skills/ors-tables-figures) · ★1.4k · 3 copies)
- **ors-theory-development**: Use when formulating the model and stating results for an Operations Research (OR) manuscript — defining the optimization/stochastic/simulation model, assumptions, and the theorems, propositions, and lemmas that carry the contribution. - **orthogonality**: Detect and resolve non-orthogonal architecture and database elements before they erode the design — a second model or table for an existing concept, one fact stored in two places, a second library or pattern for a concern already covered (HTTP client, state, auth, pagination, error envelope, charts), imports across bounded contexts, new dependency cycles, a service writing another context's tables, and code in the wrong context. Use before adding a model, table, column, library, API client wrapper, error handler, or cross-module import; when someone asks "do we already have a customer model",… ([Kaakati/sdh-claude-skills](https://github.com/Kaakati/sdh-claude-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/orthogonality) · ★2)
- **orthogonality-principle**: Use when designing modules, APIs, and system architecture requiring independent, non-overlapping components where changes in one don't affect others. ([TheBushidoCollective/han](https://github.com/TheBushidoCollective/han/tree/HEAD/plugins/core/skills/orthogonality-principle) · ★201 · 3 copies)
- **orthogonalize-pr**: Identify and separate orthogonal (independent) change sets within a decomposed PR. Creates branches for each set that could theoretically be reviewed or merged separately. ([corygabrielsen/skills](https://github.com/corygabrielsen/skills/tree/HEAD/orthogonalize-pr) · ★2)
- **orthographic-registration**: Register front, side, back, and top orthographic reference views into a shared Blender coordinate contract. - **orthography-check**: Session E of the Libro de Mormón 1920 transcription workflow — runs the orthography/spellcheck review for a page and decides on permitted-words vs. errors-in-1920 entries. Use when the user asks for an "orthography check for page NNN." ([obarrr/ldmtext](https://github.com/obarrr/ldmtext/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/orthography-check) · ★1)
- **orthogroup-lca-annotation**: Use when you have OrthoFinder (or eggNOG/PLAZA/orthomcl) orthogroup results for a query species (e.g. Danio rerio) and need per-orthogroup gene ages (LCA classes) or continuity scores of species representation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/orthogroup-lca-annotation))
- **orthogroup-locus-synteny-analysis**: Use when Scoary2 (or Scoary) mGWAS hits need genomic-context validation — e.g. checking whether trait-associated orthogenes are adjacent, syntenic, or disrupted (transposases/frameshifts) in positive vs. negative isolates. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/orthogroup-locus-synteny-analysis))
- **ortholog-abundance-correction**: Use when a KO-level abundance table from shotgun metagenomic read mapping needs normalization before pathway aggregation, and per-genome copy-number values (not raw relative abundances) are required. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/ortholog-abundance-correction))
- **ortholog-database-construction**: Use when building a KO/protein ortholog reference from hundreds of eukaryote proteomes to support read-to-ortholog mapping for non-model species RNA-seq, instead of per-species transcript assembly and annotation transfer. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/ortholog-database-construction))
- **ortholog-finder**: Anchor-first ortholog discovery between two genomes with MCScan/jcvi and LAST. Use when an agent needs a reusable Snakemake workflow that converts GFF3 to BED, checks BED/FASTA ID consistency, runs jcvi.compara.catalog ortholog, resolves query genes to subject ortholog candidates from .anchors first, optionally falls back to .last.filtered, and handles gene IDs that differ from transcript/protein FASTA IDs. ([biojiayuxin/potato-agent](https://github.com/biojiayuxin/potato-agent/tree/HEAD/skills/potato-knowledge-bioinformatics/ortholog-finder) · ★1)
- **ortholog-gene-matching-with-blast**: Use when matching cell types across a species pair requires gene/gene correspondences and a simple sequence-alignment-based ortholog map (e.g., BLAST one-to-one matches) is acceptable, or when benchmarking against embedding-based gene matching. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/ortholog-gene-matching-with-blast))
- **ortholog-group-clustering**: Use when building or extending a protein ortholog database for non-model eukaryotic species (e.g., species lacking a reference transcriptome) so reads can be quantified against ortholog groups instead of transcript IDs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/ortholog-group-clustering))
- **ortholog-group-construction-o2o-he-sh**: Use when preparing raw scRNA-seq count matrices from multiple species for integration and you must define homologous gene groups (one-to-one, one-to-many, many-to-many) before concatenating counts into a shared feature matrix. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/ortholog-group-construction-o2o-he-sh))
- **ortholog-group-differential-expression-analysis**: Use when differential expression has been called on ortholog-group IDs (e.g. via Seq2Fun/ExpressAnalyst for species lacking a reference transcriptome) and you must validate whether the significant DEG list is inflated by reads from one gene mapping to different ortholog IDs in different species. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/ortholog-group-differential-expression-analysis))
- **ortholog-hmm-gene-prediction**: Use when you have assembled fungal genomes (high-quality or draft) and need to extract single-copy marker gene sequences to build a taxonomic marker catalog or assess genome completeness across a strain collection. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/ortholog-hmm-gene-prediction))
- **ortholog-inference**: --&gt; ([mdbabumiamssm/AI-Agentic-Skills-by-Dr.-Mia](https://github.com/mdbabumiamssm/AI-Agentic-Skills-by-Dr.-Mia/tree/HEAD/Skills/Population_Genetics/comparative-genomics/ortholog-inference) · ★7)
- **ortholog-marker-accuracy-scoring**: Use when recovered marker FASTA sequences from genome-skimming retrieval runs (e.g. Patchwork, ALiBaSeq, aTRAM 2 outputs) must be quantitatively scored against a curated USCO reference set to compare accuracy across programs or coverages. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/ortholog-marker-accuracy-scoring))
- **ortholog-marker-retrieval-from-genomic-assemblies**: Use when you need to mine (near-)universal single-copy ortholog markers from a de novo short-read assembly (e.g. SPAdes contigs) or raw WGS reads for phylogenomics, with DNA-to-protein similarity search against a curated OrthoDB-based USCO reference set. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/ortholog-marker-retrieval-from-genomic-assemblies))
- **orthologous-cluster-count-validation**: Use when you have run (or are about to trust) all-vs-all BLASTP + modified Jaccard clustering on the VIRGO non-redundant gene catalog and must verify the resulting VOG cluster tables are complete and correctly merged. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/orthologous-cluster-count-validation))
- **orthologous-gene-anchoring**: Use when scRNA-seq datasets from different plant species must be integrated onto a shared UMAP/embedding while keeping cell identities consistent and detecting species-specific or conserved (e.g. meristem) cell types. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/orthologous-gene-anchoring))
- **orthologous-group-lca-gene-age-interpretation**: Use when a target species' genes need evolutionary age classes derived from precomputed or newly computed orthologous groups (OrthoFinder, eggNOG v6, PLAZA v5), e.g. to build an orthomap or count species gene-age coverage. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/orthologous-group-lca-gene-age-interpretation))
- **orthologous-promoter-region-verification**: Use when a GWAS SNP or query regulatory element has been mapped across species (e.g., human hg38 to mouse mm10 via mm10.hg38.r.chain) and you need positive-control verification that a promoter-region SNP maps to the orthologous promoter. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/orthologous-promoter-region-verification))
- **orthologous-region-mapping-chip-seq**: Use when you need to identify orthologous non-coding regulatory elements across species (e.g., human-mouse or rat-human ENCODE ChIP-seq/DNase-seq peaks) and existing sequence-conservation liftOver suffers from multi-mapping, low precision, or low mapping rate. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/orthologous-region-mapping-chip-seq))
- **orthologous-regulatory-element-mapping**: Use when you need to lift over orthologous regulatory elements (TF ChIP-seq, DNase-seq, DNA methylation peaks) between species, e.g. mouse mm10 to human hg38, and reduce multi-mapping with functional-genomic similarity scoring. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/orthologous-regulatory-element-mapping))
- **orthology-based-reaction-annotation-transfer**: Use when building a draft GSMN for a species with a genome annotation but no dedicated metabolic model, using curated reference algal/plant networks as orthology donors (e.g., A. thaliana, G. sulphuraria, E. siliculosus → C. crispus). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/orthology-based-reaction-annotation-transfer))
- **orthology-comparative-genomics**: Use when inferring orthogroups, gene-family gain and loss, synteny, or whole-genome duplication across species. Not for tree building (phylogenetic-inference) or selection tests (species-tree-and-selection). ([oumarkme/research_agents](https://github.com/oumarkme/research_agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/bioinfo-lab/skills/orthology-comparative-genomics))
- **orthomap-gene-age-coverage-counting**: Use when the task requires quantifying species coverage of precomputed gene age / orthomap assignments in oggmap, e.g. verifying that oggmap covers 317 (Ensembl release-110), 1322 (eggNOG v6), and 98 dicots + 52 monocots (PLAZA v5) eukaryotic species. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/orthomap-gene-age-coverage-counting))
- **orthomap-species-membership-mapping**: Use when you have an orthomap plus per-orthogroup species-membership table from OrthoFinder/oggmap and need lineage-continuity scores per orthogroup to spot candidate horizontal gene transfer along the LCA-to-focal-species path. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/orthomap-species-membership-mapping))
- **orthomosaic-processing**: Process a folder of drone photos into an orthomosaic (a georeferenced aerial map), plus a surface model and 3D reconstruction, using OpenDroneMap. Use this whenever a drone pilot wants to stitch flight photos into a map, build an orthomosaic or orthophoto, generate a DSM/elevation model or point cloud from drone imagery, "make a map from my drone photos", or process a photogrammetry flight — even if they don't name a specific tool. ([neblihq/drone-skills](https://github.com/neblihq/drone-skills/tree/HEAD/orthomosaic-processing))
